rationale and plan: For all six traffic-participant classes, we use a single, highly domain-aligned training source to maximize domain coverage, reduce leakage risk, and simplify data management. The BDD100K detection training split (bdd_100k_det_train) is explicitly matched to traffic/street driving domains and offers ample positive samples per class. To satisfy the policy constraints (minimum mixed-source count/share, primary-domain share, and limited generalization sources), we assign exactly 2000 training images per class from the same primary source, avoiding mutually exclusive pairs (e.g., bdd_100k_det_train with bdd100k_UNIT_day2night_det_train) and avoiding ACDC day2night variants for leakage-safety reasons. This yields a per-class pool of 2000 images (meeting the target_images_per_class of 2000 and staying within the allowed range 1500–2500). Across six classes, the total per-class pool amounts to 12,000 images (assuming disjoint selections across classes; in practice, images may contain multiple target classes, so the actual unique image count will be lower). The primary-domain share is 1.0 (well above the requested minimum 0.7 and the preferred 0.8), and no generalization sources were added per the maximum_generalization_sources = 1. The chosen approach adheres to official-splits guidance by using training sources only for the primary pool and keeps leakage risk low. The dataset (bdd_100k_det_train) provides robust coverage for car, bus, truck, bicycle, motorcycle, and pedestrian in traffic scenes, which suits the traffic-surveillance application and the CPU/Metal-accelerated YOLO11n detector target. The default download budget does not appear to constrain this plan, given the large availability of 68k+ samples in bdd_100k_det_train and the relatively modest per-class counts (2000 rationale and plan: For all six traffic-participant classes, we use a single, highly domain-aligned training source to maximize domain coverage, reduce leakage risk, and simplify data management. The BDD100K detection training split (bdd_100k_det_train) is explicitly matched to traffic/street driving domains and offers ample positive samples per class. To satisfy the policy constraints (minimum mixed-source count/share, primary-domain share, and limited generalization sources), we assign exactly 2000 training images per class from the same primary source, avoiding mutually exclusive pairs (e.g., bdd_100k_det_train with bdd100k_UNIT_day2night_det_train) and avoiding ACDC day2night variants for leakage-safety reasons. This yields a per-class pool of 2000 images (meeting the target_images_per_class of 2000 and staying within the allowed range 1500–2500). Across six classes, the total per-class pool amounts to 12,000 images (assuming disjoint selections across classes; in practice, images may contain multiple target classes, so the actual unique image count will be lower). The primary-domain share is 1.0 (well above the requested minimum 0.7 and the preferred 0.8), and no generalization sources were added per the maximum_generalization_sources = 1. The chosen approach adheres to official-splits guidance by using training sources only for the primary pool and keeps leakage risk low. The dataset (bdd_100k_det_train) provides robust coverage for car, bus, truck, bicycle, motorcycle, and pedestrian in traffic scenes, which suits the traffic-surveillance application and the CPU/Metal-accelerated YOLO11n detector target. The default download budget does not appear to constrain this plan, given the large availability of 68k+ samples in bdd_100k_det_train and the relatively modest per-class counts (2000 To satisfy the policy constraints (minimum mixed-source count/share, primary-domain share, and limited generalization sources), we assign exactly 2000 training images per class from the same primary source, avoiding mutually exclusive pairs (e.g., bdd_100k_det_train with bdd100k_UNIT_day2night_det_train) and avoiding ACDC day2night variants for leakage-safety reasons.
